\index{call\_rcu@{call\_rcu}!tree.c@{tree.c}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{call\_rcu()}{call\_rcu()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily void call\+\_\+rcu (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{include_2linux_2types_8h_a31f0e635598a9df4d5c86eb0ebe295ea}{rcu\+\_\+head}} $\ast$}]{head,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{include_2linux_2types_8h_a3fb9999f44970b426981cee0b3d005ef}{rcu\+\_\+callback\+\_\+t}}}]{func }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

\mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_a094a548179ef967658b67414a0c91d32}{call\+\_\+rcu()}} -\/ Queue an R\+CU callback for invocation after a grace period. @head\+: structure to be used for queueing the R\+CU updates. @func\+: actual callback function to be invoked after the grace period

\mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} callback function will be invoked some time after a full grace period elapses, in other words after all pre-\/existing R\+CU read-\/side critical sections have completed. However, the callback function might well execute concurrently with R\+CU read-\/side critical sections that started after \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_a094a548179ef967658b67414a0c91d32}{call\+\_\+rcu()}} was invoked.

R\+CU read-\/side critical sections are delimited by \mbox{\hyperlink{rcupdate_8h_a039341af5d714114b6c2eb8e6ed305e6}{rcu\+\_\+read\+\_\+lock()}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{rcupdate_8h_a07db93454b6994cf0af302584e84ffb9}{rcu\+\_\+read\+\_\+unlock()}}, and may be nested. In addition, but only in v5.\+0 and later, regions of code across which interrupts, preemption, or softirqs have been disabled also serve as R\+CU read-\/side critical sections. This includes hardware interrupt handlers, softirq handlers, and N\+MI handlers.

Note that all C\+P\+Us must agree that the grace period extended beyond all pre-\/existing R\+CU read-\/side critical section. On systems with more than one C\+PU, this means that when \char`\"{}func()\char`\"{} is invoked, each C\+PU is guaranteed to have executed a full memory barrier since the end of its last R\+CU read-\/side critical section whose beginning preceded the call to \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_a094a548179ef967658b67414a0c91d32}{call\+\_\+rcu()}}. It also means that each C\+PU executing an R\+CU read-\/side critical section that continues beyond the start of \char`\"{}func()\char`\"{} must have executed a memory barrier after the \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_a094a548179ef967658b67414a0c91d32}{call\+\_\+rcu()}} but before the beginning of that R\+CU read-\/side critical section. Note that these guarantees include C\+P\+Us that are offline, idle, or executing in user mode, as well as C\+P\+Us that are executing in the kernel.

Furthermore, if C\+PU A invoked \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_a094a548179ef967658b67414a0c91d32}{call\+\_\+rcu()}} and C\+PU B invoked the resulting R\+CU callback function \char`\"{}func()\char`\"{}, then both C\+PU A and C\+PU B are guaranteed to execute a full memory barrier during the time interval between the call to \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_a094a548179ef967658b67414a0c91d32}{call\+\_\+rcu()}} and the invocation of \char`\"{}func()\char`\"{} -- even if C\+PU A and C\+PU B are the same C\+PU (but again only if the system has more than one C\+PU).

Implementation of these memory-\/ordering guarantees is described here\+: Documentation/\+R\+C\+U/\+Design/\+Memory-\/\+Ordering/\+Tree-\/\+R\+C\+U-\/\+Memory-\/\+Ordering.\+rst. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

\index{tree.c@{tree.c}!cond\_synchronize\_rcu@{cond\_synchronize\_rcu}}
\index{cond\_synchronize\_rcu@{cond\_synchronize\_rcu}!tree.c@{tree.c}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{cond\_synchronize\_rcu()}{cond\_synchronize\_rcu()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily void cond\+\_\+synchronize\+\_\+rcu (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{unsigned long}]{oldstate }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

cond\+\_\+synchronize\+\_\+rcu -\/ Conditionally wait for an R\+CU grace period

@oldstate\+: value from \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_ab8d6f66e4a6e1a290d3b466bdd772fa8}{get\+\_\+state\+\_\+synchronize\+\_\+rcu()}} or \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_a5edbd82d3571c90c9ebcba4fd10e01d4}{start\+\_\+poll\+\_\+synchronize\+\_\+rcu()}}

If a full R\+CU grace period has elapsed since the earlier call to \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_ab8d6f66e4a6e1a290d3b466bdd772fa8}{get\+\_\+state\+\_\+synchronize\+\_\+rcu()}} or \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_a5edbd82d3571c90c9ebcba4fd10e01d4}{start\+\_\+poll\+\_\+synchronize\+\_\+rcu()}}, just return. Otherwise, invoke \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_a2a82b0ecedfdd3957789e90bdfd47756}{synchronize\+\_\+rcu()}} to wait for a full grace period.

Yes, this function does not take counter wrap into account. But counter wrap is harmless. If the counter wraps, we have waited for more than 2 billion grace periods (and way more on a 64-\/bit system!), so waiting for one additional grace period should be just fine.

This function provides the same memory-\/ordering guarantees that would be provided by a \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_a2a82b0ecedfdd3957789e90bdfd47756}{synchronize\+\_\+rcu()}} that was invoked at the call to the function that provided @oldstate, and that returned at the end of this function. get\+\_\+state\+\_\+synchronize\+\_\+rcu -\/ Snapshot current R\+CU state

\index{tree.c@{tree.c}!poll\_state\_synchronize\_rcu@{poll\_state\_synchronize\_rcu}}
\index{poll\_state\_synchronize\_rcu@{poll\_state\_synchronize\_rcu}!tree.c@{tree.c}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{poll\_state\_synchronize\_rcu()}{poll\_state\_synchronize\_rcu()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \mbox{\hyperlink{include_2linux_2types_8h_afaa87723b8417d40fcf45b7330261ef9}{bool}} poll\+\_\+state\+\_\+synchronize\+\_\+rcu (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{unsigned long}]{oldstate }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

poll\+\_\+state\+\_\+synchronize\+\_\+rcu -\/ Conditionally wait for an R\+CU grace period

@oldstate\+: value from \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_ab8d6f66e4a6e1a290d3b466bdd772fa8}{get\+\_\+state\+\_\+synchronize\+\_\+rcu()}} or \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_a5edbd82d3571c90c9ebcba4fd10e01d4}{start\+\_\+poll\+\_\+synchronize\+\_\+rcu()}}

If a full R\+CU grace period has elapsed since the earlier call from which oldstate was obtained, return @true, otherwise return @false. If @false is returned, it is the caller\textquotesingle{}s responsibility to invoke this function later on until it does return @true. Alternatively, the caller can explicitly wait for a grace period, for example, by passing @oldstate to \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_a29db2a365de14ef720fd3557734b6ec7}{cond\+\_\+synchronize\+\_\+rcu()}} or by directly invoking \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_a2a82b0ecedfdd3957789e90bdfd47756}{synchronize\+\_\+rcu()}}.

Yes, this function does not take counter wrap into account. But counter wrap is harmless. If the counter wraps, we have waited for more than 2 billion grace periods (and way more on a 64-\/bit system!). Those needing to keep oldstate values for very long time periods (many hours even on 32-\/bit systems) should check them occasionally and either refresh them or set a flag indicating that the grace period has completed.

This function provides the same memory-\/ordering guarantees that would be provided by a \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_a2a82b0ecedfdd3957789e90bdfd47756}{synchronize\+\_\+rcu()}} that was invoked at the call to the function that provided @oldstate, and that returned at the end of this function. \index{tree.c@{tree.c}!rcu\_barrier@{rcu\_barrier}}
\index{rcu\_barrier@{rcu\_barrier}!tree.c@{tree.c}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{rcu\_barrier()}{rcu\_barrier()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily void rcu\+\_\+barrier (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{void}]{ }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

rcu\+\_\+barrier -\/ Wait until all in-\/flight \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_a094a548179ef967658b67414a0c91d32}{call\+\_\+rcu()}} callbacks complete.

Note that this primitive does not necessarily wait for an R\+CU grace period to complete. For example, if there are no R\+CU callbacks queued anywhere in the system, then \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_a7ac3474e3971b47e01b1a5f5c590d8ee}{rcu\+\_\+barrier()}} is within its rights to return immediately, without waiting for anything, much less an R\+CU grace period. \doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{rcu\_idle\_enter()}{rcu\_idle\_enter()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily void rcu\+\_\+idle\+\_\+enter (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{void}]{ }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

rcu\+\_\+idle\+\_\+enter -\/ inform R\+CU that current C\+PU is entering idle

Enter idle mode, in other words, -\/leave-\/ the mode in which R\+CU read-\/side critical sections can occur. (Though R\+CU read-\/side critical sections can occur in irq handlers in idle, a possibility handled by \mbox{\hyperlink{softirq_8c_a9b4b9fc71a87343ec21c5be6f5b77713}{irq\+\_\+enter()}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{softirq_8c_ae025cdfd62a31b6df5f6d83a494cbe60}{irq\+\_\+exit()}}.)

If you add or remove a call to \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_aabd0d60f3fe5c1ca0e601af88936201b}{rcu\+\_\+idle\+\_\+enter()}}, be sure to test with C\+O\+N\+F\+I\+G\+\_\+\+R\+C\+U\+\_\+\+E\+Q\+S\+\_\+\+D\+E\+B\+UG=y. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

rcu\+\_\+irq\+\_\+enter -\/ inform R\+CU that current C\+PU is entering irq away from idle

Enter an interrupt handler, which might possibly result in exiting idle mode, in other words, entering the mode in which read-\/side critical sections can occur. \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} caller must have disabled interrupts.

Note that the Linux kernel is fully capable of entering an interrupt handler that it never exits, for example when doing upcalls to user mode! This code assumes that the idle loop never does upcalls to user mode. If your architecture\textquotesingle{}s idle loop does do upcalls to user mode (or does anything else that results in unbalanced calls to the \mbox{\hyperlink{softirq_8c_a9b4b9fc71a87343ec21c5be6f5b77713}{irq\+\_\+enter()}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{softirq_8c_ae025cdfd62a31b6df5f6d83a494cbe60}{irq\+\_\+exit()}} functions), R\+CU will give you what you deserve, good and hard. But very infrequently and irreproducibly.

Use things like work queues to work around this limitation.

You have been warned.

If you add or remove a call to \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_a8cebd94ac3da6cb4d6b467e2eb8b0751}{rcu\+\_\+irq\+\_\+enter()}}, be sure to test with C\+O\+N\+F\+I\+G\+\_\+\+R\+C\+U\+\_\+\+E\+Q\+S\+\_\+\+D\+E\+B\+UG=y. Here is the call graph for this function\+:
% FIG 153

rcu\+\_\+irq\+\_\+exit -\/ inform R\+CU that current C\+PU is exiting irq towards idle

Exit from an interrupt handler, which might possibly result in entering idle mode, in other words, leaving the mode in which read-\/side critical sections can occur. \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} caller must have disabled interrupts.

This code assumes that the idle loop never does anything that might result in unbalanced calls to \mbox{\hyperlink{softirq_8c_a9b4b9fc71a87343ec21c5be6f5b77713}{irq\+\_\+enter()}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{softirq_8c_ae025cdfd62a31b6df5f6d83a494cbe60}{irq\+\_\+exit()}}. If your architecture\textquotesingle{}s idle loop violates this assumption, R\+CU will give you what you deserve, good and hard. But very infrequently and irreproducibly.

Use things like work queues to work around this limitation.

You have been warned.

If you add or remove a call to \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_a5d2774cea1c93603269f5d4df9adec0f}{rcu\+\_\+irq\+\_\+exit()}}, be sure to test with C\+O\+N\+F\+I\+G\+\_\+\+R\+C\+U\+\_\+\+E\+Q\+S\+\_\+\+D\+E\+B\+UG=y. Here is the call graph for this function\+:
% FIG 157

rcu\+\_\+is\+\_\+watching -\/ see if R\+CU thinks that the current C\+PU is not idle

Return true if R\+CU is watching the running C\+PU, which means that this C\+PU can safely enter R\+CU read-\/side critical sections. In other words, if the current C\+PU is not in its idle loop or is in an interrupt or N\+MI handler, return true.

Make notrace because it can be called by the internal functions of ftrace, and making this notrace removes unnecessary recursion calls. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

rcu\+\_\+nmi\+\_\+enter -\/ inform R\+CU of entry to N\+MI context

If the C\+PU was idle from R\+CU\textquotesingle{}s viewpoint, update rdp-\/$>$dynticks and rdp-\/$>$dynticks\+\_\+nmi\+\_\+nesting to let the R\+CU grace-\/period handling know that the C\+PU is active. This implementation permits nested N\+M\+Is, as long as the nesting level does not overflow an int. (You will probably run out of stack space first.)

If you add or remove a call to \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_a7755c9f7d4d73ceb9126d32e01654cfd}{rcu\+\_\+nmi\+\_\+enter()}}, be sure to test with C\+O\+N\+F\+I\+G\+\_\+\+R\+C\+U\+\_\+\+E\+Q\+S\+\_\+\+D\+E\+B\+UG=y. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

rcu\+\_\+nmi\+\_\+exit -\/ inform R\+CU of exit from N\+MI context

If we are returning from the outermost N\+MI handler that interrupted an R\+C\+U-\/idle period, update rdp-\/$>$dynticks and rdp-\/$>$dynticks\+\_\+nmi\+\_\+nesting to let the R\+CU grace-\/period handling know that the C\+PU is back to being R\+C\+U-\/idle.

\index{tree.c@{tree.c}!start\_poll\_synchronize\_rcu@{start\_poll\_synchronize\_rcu}}
\index{start\_poll\_synchronize\_rcu@{start\_poll\_synchronize\_rcu}!tree.c@{tree.c}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{start\_poll\_synchronize\_rcu()}{start\_poll\_synchronize\_rcu()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily unsigned long start\+\_\+poll\+\_\+synchronize\+\_\+rcu (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{void}]{ }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

start\+\_\+poll\+\_\+synchronize\+\_\+rcu -\/ Snapshot and start R\+CU grace period

Returns a cookie that is used by a later call to \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_a29db2a365de14ef720fd3557734b6ec7}{cond\+\_\+synchronize\+\_\+rcu()}} or \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_ad959d1ea0fb450870102e2473014a27e}{poll\+\_\+state\+\_\+synchronize\+\_\+rcu()}} to determine whether or not a full grace period has elapsed in the meantime. If the needed grace period is not already slated to start, notifies R\+CU core of the need for that grace period.

Interrupts must be enabled for the case where it is necessary to awaken the grace-\/period kthread. Here is the call graph for this function\+:
% FIG 217
\mbox{\Hypertarget{tree_8c_a533552197c6c6b7572a7cd5928f2505f}\label{tree_8c_a533552197c6c6b7572a7cd5928f2505f}}

\mbox{\Hypertarget{tree_8c_a2a82b0ecedfdd3957789e90bdfd47756}\label{tree_8c_a2a82b0ecedfdd3957789e90bdfd47756}} 
\index{tree.c@{tree.c}!synchronize\_rcu@{synchronize\_rcu}}
\index{synchronize\_rcu@{synchronize\_rcu}!tree.c@{tree.c}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{synchronize\_rcu()}{synchronize\_rcu()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily void synchronize\+\_\+rcu (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{void}]{ }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

synchronize\+\_\+rcu -\/ wait until a grace period has elapsed.

Control will return to the caller some time after a full grace period has elapsed, in other words after all currently executing R\+CU read-\/side critical sections have completed. Note, however, that upon return from \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_a2a82b0ecedfdd3957789e90bdfd47756}{synchronize\+\_\+rcu()}}, the caller might well be executing concurrently with new R\+CU read-\/side critical sections that began while \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_a2a82b0ecedfdd3957789e90bdfd47756}{synchronize\+\_\+rcu()}} was waiting.

R\+CU read-\/side critical sections are delimited by \mbox{\hyperlink{rcupdate_8h_a039341af5d714114b6c2eb8e6ed305e6}{rcu\+\_\+read\+\_\+lock()}} and \mbox{\hyperlink{rcupdate_8h_a07db93454b6994cf0af302584e84ffb9}{rcu\+\_\+read\+\_\+unlock()}}, and may be nested. In addition, but only in v5.\+0 and later, regions of code across which interrupts, preemption, or softirqs have been disabled also serve as R\+CU read-\/side critical sections. This includes hardware interrupt handlers, softirq handlers, and N\+MI handlers.

Note that this guarantee implies further memory-\/ordering guarantees. On systems with more than one C\+PU, when \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_a2a82b0ecedfdd3957789e90bdfd47756}{synchronize\+\_\+rcu()}} returns, each C\+PU is guaranteed to have executed a full memory barrier since the end of its last R\+CU read-\/side critical section whose beginning preceded the call to \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_a2a82b0ecedfdd3957789e90bdfd47756}{synchronize\+\_\+rcu()}}. In addition, each C\+PU having an R\+CU read-\/side critical section that extends beyond the return from \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_a2a82b0ecedfdd3957789e90bdfd47756}{synchronize\+\_\+rcu()}} is guaranteed to have executed a full memory barrier after the beginning of \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_a2a82b0ecedfdd3957789e90bdfd47756}{synchronize\+\_\+rcu()}} and before the beginning of that R\+CU read-\/side critical section. Note that these guarantees include C\+P\+Us that are offline, idle, or executing in user mode, as well as C\+P\+Us that are executing in the kernel.

Furthermore, if C\+PU A invoked \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_a2a82b0ecedfdd3957789e90bdfd47756}{synchronize\+\_\+rcu()}}, which returned to its caller on C\+PU B, then both C\+PU A and C\+PU B are guaranteed to have executed a full memory barrier during the execution of \mbox{\hyperlink{tree_8c_a2a82b0ecedfdd3957789e90bdfd47756}{synchronize\+\_\+rcu()}} -- even if C\+PU A and C\+PU B are the same C\+PU (but again only if the system has more than one C\+PU).

Implementation of these memory-\/ordering guarantees is described here\+: Documentation/\+R\+C\+U/\+Design/\+Memory-\/\+Ordering/\+Tree-\/\+R\+C\+U-\/\+Memory-\/\+Ordering.\+rst. Here is the call graph for this function\+:
